A simpler powerful method: Use **iterative thresholding / reweighting**:
Minimize max c_k. We can use **subgradient descent with adaptive step and Nesterov acceleration**. Since objective is max of many quadratics, we can use the following first-order method for minimizing max of quadratics over simplex:
- Maintain current a.
- Compute active weights w_k ∝ exp(β c_k) (softmax) over all lags (or top K).
- Gradient g = 2 A_w a.
- Step a <- project_simplex(a - η g).
- Use line search on soft objective F_β or max.
- Update β increasing (annealing).
This is like minimizing smooth approximation. Could be efficient if we compute A_w a fast using convolution? A_w a = weighted sum of shifts of a. Instead of O(K n), we can compute via convolution? For each lag k, shift a by k weighted w_k. This is equivalent to (w * a)? Let's see (A_k a)_i = a_{k-i} if valid. Weighted sum ∑ w_k a_{k-i} = convolution of w (length 2n-1?) with a? Let w_k for k=0..2n-2. Define h_i = ∑_k w_k a_{k-i}. This is cross-correlation of a with w. Can compute via FFT: h = ? If w is kernel, h = correlate(a,w). Let's design **fictitious play** carefully. This seems promising and state-of-art.
Pseudo:
```
def fictitious_play(a_start, budget, solver, max_iters, use_avg=True, print...):
   n=len(a); a=normalize positive;
   a_avg=a.copy()
   best_a=a; best_score=score(a)
   history=[]
   t0=time
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time > budget-break
      # Solve LP best response to a (or a_avg? maybe use a_cur)
      b = lp_best_response(a)
      if b None: break
      # Line search from a to b
      for start in [a, a_avg]:
          t_best, a_trial, score_trial = line_search_convex_combo(start,b,n)
          if score < best: update
          # accept best direction as new a?
      a = best_trial (or start+alpha b)
      # Update average with exponential decay: alpha=2/(it+2) or adaptive
      a_avg = (1-alpha)*a_avg + alpha*a? Or average of b values: a_avg = (1 - alpha)*a_avg + alpha*b
      # Check a_avg
      # If no improvement for many, use current = a_avg
   return best
```

LP via scipy:
```
import scipy.optimize as opt
from scipy.sparse import csc_matrix, diags, hstack

def lp_best_response_scipy(a, max_iters=...):
   n=len(a)
   m=2*n-1
   data=np.repeat(a, n)
   indices=... # precompute? We can have global cache by n.
   indptr=np.arange(n+1)*n
   T=csc_matrix((data, indices, indptr), shape=(m,n))
   A_ub = hstack([T, diags([-1]*m, shape=(m,m))], format='csc') # diagonal for t variable length m
   A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1
   b_ub = np.zeros(m)
   b_eq = np.ones(1)
   bounds = [(0,None)]*n + [(None,None)]
   res=opt.linprog(np.zeros(n+1), A_ub=A_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_ub=b_ub, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
```
But constructing diags each time and hstack okay. We can precompute A_eq and bounds per n. A_ub t diagonal precompute? Combine T and diag: A_ub sparse = T with extra column. We can build full A_ub directly:
- Data: T_data repeated (1M); t_data = -np.ones(m) (2k). Indices: T_indices (1M); t_indices = np.arange(m). Indptr for m+1 columns: For columns 0..n-1, each n nonzeros => (j+1)*n. Last column m nonzero. So indptr = np.concatenate([np.arange(n+1)*n, [n*n + m]]) shape n+2? Actually columns count n+1, indptr length n+2? Approximate best response via iterative updates:
Given a, we want b minimizing max_k conv(a,b)_k. This is a linear minimax problem. We can use multiplicative weights on constraints: start b uniform. For iter s, compute active weights over k where conv(a,b_t) high, update b_i *= exp(-η gradient w.r.t b at active?) Since objective max linear in b, use MW: b_{t+1,i} = b_{t,i} exp(-eta ∑_{k active} w_k coeff_{k,i}) / sum. Gradient wrt b_i = ∑_k w_k a_{k-i} (similar h). This is like projected subgradient for LP. Could converge in O(sqrt(K) log) iterations, each O(n log n) if using FFT. This may be a good approximation and faster than LP. We can run ~100 MW iterations to get b, then line search. For fixed a, exact LP best response maybe not needed; approximate may suffice.

Let's consider **active-set quadratic optimization for a given set of lags**. Suppose active peaks K. The objective approximates max of K quadratics. We can use Newton's method for KKT: minimize max by solving system where active constraints equal T and gradient combination. At optimum for a subset active with weights λ (sum1), there exists T such that:
q_k(a)=T for active k.
2 ∑ λ_k A_k a = μ 1 + ν (ν_i≥0 for inactive a_i).
a_i≥0, sum1.
This is like finding a stationary point. We can iterate:
- Compute active peaks and λ.
- Form B = ∑ λ_k A_k.
- Find μ and ν such that a solves B a = μ/2 * 1 + ν/2? This is a support vector machine dual. If we ignore inequalities, solve linear system? B may be indefinite, but maybe. Hard.

def combo_score(u,v,t):

Coordinate descent parameter details:
Given a, conv, S, i, v. We compute:
- k_lo = i, k_hi = min(i+n-1,2n-2), n_aff = k_hi-k_lo+1.
- partners = np.arange(k_lo,k_hi+1)-i (0..n_aff-1) because standard conv: c_k = ∑_{j=k-n+1..k} a_j a_{k-j}; coordinate i affected k where k-i in [0,n-1]. partner index = k-i. Good.
- unaff_max = max(conv[:k_lo], conv[k_hi+1:]) but for objective full max, if k_lo=0 include none; if k_hi last include none.
- c_vals: For each idx, k=k_lo+idx.
  If k == 2*i: c = conv[k] - v^2 (because square term)
  else: c = conv[k] - 2*v*a[partner]
Actually if k==i? partner 0, if k=2i not special. For k where partner = i (i.e. k=2i), the formula `2*v*a[partner]` would be 2v^2, but correct delta for square is x^2 - v^2. So override.
- new conv at k = c + 2*x*a[partner] (or c + x^2).
- new sum = S + x - v.
- score = 2n*max(unaff_max, max(new_aff))/S_new^2.
Need ensure S_new>0.01? In internal normalized S=1, delta > -v, S>=0. We'll keep positive.

Now first-order softmax projected gradient. Could help if coordinate stuck. Implement:
```
def softmax_dir_and_grad(a, beta, top_k=None):
    conv=fast_convolve(a)
    c=conv
    maxc=c.max()
    e=np.exp(beta*(c-maxc))
    w=e/e.sum()
    h=weighted_shift(a,w) # length n
    grad=2*h
    # soft obj = (maxc + log(e.sum())/beta)
    return w,h,soft
```
Weighted_shift with FFT as derived.
```
def weighted_shift(a,w):
    n=len(a); m=len(w) # 2n-1
    R=w[::-1]
    fft_len=1 << ((n + m -1)-1).bit_length()
    A=rfft(a, fft_len)
    B=rfft(R, fft_len)
    conv=irfft(A*B, fft_len)
    # h_i = conv[2n-2 - i] for i=0..n-1
    return conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Need check length: n+m-1 = 3n-2. `irfft` returns fft_len. We take indices n-1..2n-2 (inclusive) length n, reverse. For n=3, conv length 7, indices 2,3,4 reversed => [4,3,2]. Good.
But if using fft_len > length, values beyond zero. Good.

Maybe weights w are length 2n-1, but for c near edges small. Use all. For memory, R length 2n-1; FFT length 3n. Good.

LP solver details with sparse A_ub:
Precompute per n:
```
def get_lp_cache(n):
  if n in cache: return
  m=2*n-1
  col_indices = np.tile(np.arange(n), n) + np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
  t_indices = np.arange(m)
  indices = np.concatenate((col_indices, t_indices))
  indptr = np.empty(n+2, dtype=np.int64)
  indptr[:-1] = np.arange(n+1, dtype=np.int64) * n
  indptr[-1] = n*n + m
  # data for A_ub: first n*n for T, then -1 for t. T data depends on a.
  cache[n]=...
```
Wait for A_ub shape (m, n+1), columns 0..n-1 each have n nonzeros, last column m. `indptr` length n+2. For j=0 start0, j=1 start n, ..., column n-1 start (n-1)n, end n*n. Then last column start n*n, end n*n+m. `indptr[0]=0, indptr[1]=n, ..., indptr[n]=n*n, indptr[n+1]=n*n+m`. So `indptr = np.arange(n+1)*n; indptr = np.append(indptr, n*n+m)`. length n+2? `np.arange(n+1)` length n+1, append 1 => n+2. Good.
Data length = n*n + m. Indices length same.
For given a, T_data = np.repeat(a, n) (column-major: a[0] repeated n, a[1] repeated n...). t_data = -np.ones(m). data = np.concatenate((T_data, t_data)). Build csc_matrix((data, indices, indptr), shape=(m,n+1)). Ensure duplicates? There are no duplicates within columns, no cross column duplicates. Good.
A_eq: dense (1,n+1), first n ones, last 0. b_eq=1.
Objective c: zeros n+1.
Bounds: b_i [0,None], t [None,None]. Use list.
Call linprog.

Potential performance: Building A_ub each LP with 1M data and indices references. Indices cached read-only; data new. csc_matrix may copy indices? It may use arrays. Fine. We can set `copy=False`. But csc expects sorted indices within columns; our indices per column are increasing (j..j+n-1) and t column increasing. Good.

**Trimming leading/trailing zeros**:
```
def trim_sequence(a):
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   # remove leading zeros less than 1e-15? If exact small, maybe keep? We can find threshold 1e-12*max.
   thresh = 1e-12 * max(1, a.max())
   lo=0; hi=len(a)
   while lo<hi and a[lo] < thresh: lo+=1
   while hi>lo and a[hi-1] < thresh: hi-=1
   b=a[lo:hi]
   # maybe if trimmed fewer than original, check score improves; if len < 2 return original?
   if len(b)>=2:
      s=b.sum(); if s>0: b=b/s; return b
   return a/s
```
If internal zeros remain, don't trim.

Detailed subgradient loop:
```
def subgradient_polish(a0, budget, beta_start, beta_end, lr0, rng):
  a=normalize; best=a; best_score=score
  it=0; no_improve=0
  while time < budget:
    conv=fast_conv(a); maxc=conv.max()
    # weights: use top K active + softmax
    K=min(50,len(conv)); peaks=argpartition(conv,-K); peaks.sort()
    # option1 uniform top K: w zeros; w[peaks]=1/K
    # option2 softmax top all: e=exp(beta(conv - conv[peaks.max])); w concentrate
    # gradient h = weighted_shift(a,w)
    h=weighted_shift(a,w)
    # try lrs around lr0: [lr*0.25,0.5,1,2,4]
    best_cand=None
    for lr in lrs:
       cand=project(a-lr*h) # h already half grad
       sc=quick_score(cand)
       if sc<score: accept
    if accept: a=cand; best update; no_improve=0; beta*=1.05; lr*=1.05
    else: no_improve++; if no_improve>100 break; lr*=0.9; beta*=1.1; maybe random restart
```
But quick_score with FFT each candidate; 5 evals per iteration; 1000 iterations ~5000 FFT, okay. However projected gradient may get stuck in shallow. Use random perturbations if no improve.

**Coordinate descent with top-level param**:
Define `coord_score(x, params)`:
```
c_lin, ap_lin, has_quad, c_quad, unaff, S, v, n = params
ns = S + x - v
if ns < 1e-12: return 1e100
m = unaff
if c_lin.size:
    val = np.max(c_lin + 2.0*ap_lin*x)
    if val > m: m = val
if has_quad:
    q = c_quad + x*x
    if q > m: m=q
return 2.0*n*m/(ns*ns)
```
But `c_lin` may contain -1e30 for quad excluded. Need ensure no overflow. If n_aff=1 and has_quad, c_lin size 1 with -1e30; `np.max(-1e30+0)` -1e30, okay.
For non-quad, c_lin = conv segment -2v*partners; all finite. `ap_lin` partners.
`prep_coord_params(a, conv, S, i)`:
```

ap = np.array(part, dtype=float) # partners indices 0..n_aff-1
c_lin = seg - 2.0*v*a[part]
has_quad = (2*i >= k_lo) and (2*i <= k_hi)
if has_quad:
   idx=2*i-k_lo
   c_quad = conv[2*i] - v*v
   # exclude quad from linear part
   c_lin = c_lin.copy(); ap = ap.copy()
   c_lin[idx] = -1e30; ap[idx]=0.0
else: c_quad=0.0
unaff = -1e30
if k_lo>0: unaff=max(unaff,float(conv[:k_lo].max()))
if k_hi<2*n-2: unaff=max(unaff,float(conv[k_hi+1:].max()))

```

Now, **pool weight optimization** implementation specifics:
```
def weight_score(lam, C, n):
    c = np.einsum('i,ijk,j->k', lam, C, lam, optimize=True)
    return 2.0*n*float(c.max())
```

```
def lp_best_response(a, timeout_sec=8):
   n=len(a)
   try:
      res=... linprog ...
      if res.status ==0 and res.x is not None: extract
      else raise
   except Exception:
      try cvxpy...
   if b is None: return None,None
   b=np.maximum(b,0); s=b.sum(); if s<1e-10 return None,None; b/=s
   # ensure sum exactly
   return b, float(max(fast_convolve(a,b)))
```

def combo_score_coeffs(t, c_u, c_v, c_uv, n):
   a=1-t
   c = a*a*c_u + 2*a*t*c_uv + t*t*c_v
   return 2*n*float(c.max())
```

**`trim_if_better`** with quick_score calls FFT. For length1024, okay. It modifies by copy.
```
def trim_if_better(a):
    a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-15: return a/...
    a=a/s; orig=quick_score(a); eps=max(1e-12*float(a.max()),1e-15)
    for _ in range(10):
       lo=0; hi=len(a)
       while lo<hi and a[lo]<eps: lo+=1
       while hi>lo and a[hi-1]<eps: hi-=1
       if lo==0 and hi==len(a): break
       b=a[lo:hi]
       if len(b)<2: break
       b=b/b.sum()
       sc=quick_score(b)
       if sc < orig - 1e-14:
           orig=sc; a=b
       else:
           break
    return a
```
If trimming leads to length1, maybe score? Could be high; keep original.

**`l_bfgs_polish` implementation**:
```
def l_bfgs_polish(a0, time_limit, beta_targets=(2.0,6.0,12.0), label=''):
   a=normalize(a0); n=len(a); cur=normalize(a); cur_score=quick_score(cur); best=cur; best_score=cur_score; t0=time
   if n<2: return best,best_score
   # y0 from cur with epsilon
   eps = 1e-12 / n
   a_safe = np.maximum(cur, eps); a_safe = a_safe / a_safe.sum(); y0=np.log(a_safe)
   bounds=[(float(np.log(eps)), 0.0)]*n
   for target in beta_targets:
      if time.time - t0 > time_limit - 10: break
      # estimate beta
      cmax_now = float(fast_convolve(cur).max())
      beta = target / max(cmax_now, 1e-12)
      if beta > 200000: beta=200000
      _LB_STATE[0]=float(beta); _LB_STATE[1]=int(n)
      # choose maxiter based on n? maybe 60 for n>1000 else 120
      maxiter = 70 if n>=1024 else 150
      try:
         res=spopt.minimize(lbfgs_objective, y0, jac=lbfgs_gradient, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxfun': maxiter*30})
         if res.x is not None:
            y_new=res.x
            # clip to bounds
            y_new=np.clip(y_new, bounds[0][0], bounds[0][1])
            a_new=softmax_from_y(y_new)
            a_new=np.maximum(a_new,0); ss=a_new.sum(); if ss>0: a_new/=ss
            sc=quick_score(a_new)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-14:
                best=a_new; best_score=sc; cur=a_new
            else:

def fictitious_play(a_start, budget, pool=None, label=''):

    best=a; best_score=quick_score(a)
    avg=a.copy(); active=a.copy()

    while time.monotonic()-t0 < budget:
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > budget-40: break  # leave time
       it +=1
       rem = budget - (time.monotonic()-t0)
       lp_timeout = min(8.0, max(2.0, rem/10.0))
       b, tval = lp_best_response(active, timeout_sec=lp_timeout)

**Flatten spectrum init**: Random phases can produce `a` with negative? We take abs. But for standard convolution, a nonnegative. Good.
```
def init_flat_spectrum(n,rng):
    n_fft=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
    n_freq=n_fft//2+1
    phases=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,n_freq)
    amps=rng.uniform(0.5,1.5,n_freq)
    spec=amps*np.exp(1j*phases)
    a=irfft(spec,n_fft)[:n]
    a=np.abs(a)
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return np.ones(n)/n
    return a/s
```
Maybe `irfft` output length n_fft; good.

### `normalize_positive`
```
def normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        n = a.size
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1,n)
    return a / s
```

### `fast_autoconv`
```
def fast_autoconv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return np.zeros(1)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fft_size = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    A = rfft(a, fft_size)
    return irfft(A * A, fft_size)[:size]
```
For n=1, size1, (size-1).bit_length() =0, fft_size=1, ok.

### `fast_conv_pair`
```
def fast_conv_pair(a,b):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); b=np.asarray(b,float)
    n=len(a); m=len(b)
    size=n+m-1
    fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    return irfft(rfft(a,fft_size)*rfft(b,fft_size), fft_size)[:size]
```

### `interpolate_seq`
```
def interpolate_seq(a,new_n):
   a=normalize_positive(a)
   if len(a)==new_n: return a.copy()
   old_x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(a))
   new_x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
   b=np.interp(new_x, old_x, a)
   b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
   s=b.sum()
   if s<1e-12: return np.ones(new_n)/new_n
   return b/s
```

### `simplex_project`
Standard. If v all negative, returns uniform? Use.
```
def simplex_project(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float)
    n=len(v)
    if n==0: return v
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.arange(1,n+1)
    cond=u*rho > (cssv - 1.0)
    if cond.any():
       idx=int(np.nonzero(cond)[0][-1])
       theta=(cssv[idx]-1.0)/(idx+1.0)
       return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
    return np.ones(n)/n
```

### `weighted_shift`
As above. Ensure `w` same length `2*n-1`. If w length different, adjust? not.
```
def weighted_shift(a,w):
   n=len(a); m=len(w)
   if m != 2*n-1: w=...? assume.
   R=w[::-1]
   size=n+m-1
   fft_size=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
   A=rfft(a,fft_size); B=rfft(R,fft_size)
   c=irfft(A*B, fft_size)
   return c[n-1:2*n-1][::-1].copy()
```
Check slice: Need length n. For n=3, c indices 2:5? c[2:5] length3 reversed. `2*n-1` is 5, exclusive -> indices2,3,4. Good. For n=1, c[0:1] [::-1]. Good.

### L-BFGS global and functions
```
_LB_STATE = [0.0, 0]
def softmax_from_y(y):
    z = y - np.max(y)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = e.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        z = z - np.max(z) # maybe
        e = np.exp(z); s=e.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(len(y), 1.0/len(y))
    return e/s
```
Objective:
```
def lbfgs_objective(y):
    beta = float(_LB_STATE[0]); n=int(_LB_STATE[1])
    a = softmax_from_y(y)
    c = fast_autoconv(a)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if beta <= 1e-3:
        M = cmax
    else:
        e = np.exp(beta*(c-cmax))
        z = float(e.sum())
        if z <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(z):
            M = cmax
        else:
            M = cmax + float(np.log(z))/beta
    return float(2.0 * n * M)
```
Gradient similar. Need if n==0 no.
```
def lbfgs_gradient(y):
   beta=...; n=...
   a=softmax_from_y(y); c=fast_autoconv(a); cmax=...
   if beta<=: w = zeros; w[argmax]=1; M=cmax
   else w=...; M=...
   h=weighted_shift(a,w)
   g=2.0*n*(h - a*M)
   # ensure finite
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): g=np.zeros(n)
   return g
```
In gradient, if beta high and e sum maybe 0? handle.

`l_bfgs_polish`: Need local `y0` from cur. If cur has zeros, eps.
```
def l_bfgs_polish(a0, time_limit, beta_targets=(2.0, 6.0, 12.0), label=''):
    cur=normalize_positive(np.asarray(a0,float)); n=len(cur)
    if n < 2 or time_limit <= 5: return cur, quick_score(cur)
    best=cur.copy(); best_sc=quick_score(cur); t0=time.monotonic()
    eps=1e-12/max(1,n)
    safe=np.maximum(cur, eps); safe=safe/safe.sum()
    y0=np.log(safe)
    bounds=[(float(np.log(eps)), 0.0)]**n  # caution: list multiplication of tuples okay, tuples immutable. `[(...)]*n`.
    for target in beta_targets:
       rem=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 10: break
       cmax = float(fast_autoconv(cur).max())
       beta = float(target) / max(cmax, 1e-12)
       if beta < 1.0: beta=1.0
       if beta > 100000.0: beta=100000.0
       _LB_STATE[0]=beta; _LB_STATE[1]=n
       maxfun = 300 if n >= 1024 else 600
       maxiter = 100 if n >= 1024 else 200
       try:
          res = spopt.minimize(lbfgs_objective, y0, jac=lbfgs_gradient, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': maxfun, 'ftol': 1e-12, 'gtol': 1e-8, 'maxls': 20})
          if res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
              y_new = np.clip(res.x, np.log(eps), 0.0)
              a_new = softmax_from_y(y_new)
              a_new = np.maximum(a_new, 0.0); ss=a_new.sum(); if ss>1e-300: a_new/=ss
              sc = quick_score(a_new)
              if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                  best = a_new.copy(); best_sc = sc; cur = a_new.copy()
                  if label: print(f"    LBFGS {label}: {best_sc:.8f} beta={beta:.0f}")
              # optionally set cur to a_new even if not best? Maybe if finite and lower than cur? best tracks. To continue from improved, if not best, cur remains. Could set cur=a_new if sc < quick_score(cur) even if not best; best already.
              else:
                  sc_cur = quick_score(cur)
                  if sc < sc_cur:
                      cur=a_new.copy()
              safe=np.maximum(cur, eps); safe=safe/safe.sum(); y0=np.log(safe)
       except Exception as e:
          if label: print(f"    LBFGS {label} error: {e}")
    best=trim_if_better(best)
    best_sc=quick_score(best)
    return best, best_sc
```
Potential: `bounds=[(low, high)] * n` okay. `low = float(np.log(eps))`. If eps underflows? n up to 4096, eps=2.4e-16, log ~-36. Good. If n=1, handled.

`maxfun` and `maxiter` maybe too small to improve; can set maxfun=500 for n large. Time? 500 evals per beta, 3 beta ->1500 FFTs, maybe 10s. Good. Use 400.

### `subgradient_candidate`

```
def subgradient_candidate(a, lrs=(0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4)):
    a=normalize_positive(a); n=len(a); if n<2: return a, quick_score(a)
    c=fast_autoconv(a)
    K=min(32, c.size)
    peaks=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    w=np.zeros(c.size); w[peaks]=1.0/len(peaks)
    h=weighted_shift(a,w)
    best=a; best_sc=quick_score(a)
    for lr in lrs:
       cand=simplex_project(a - lr*h)
       sc=quick_score(cand)
       if sc < best_sc: best=cand; best_sc=sc
    return best,best_sc
```
Should we use gradient 2h but scale lr; okay.

### `line_search_combo`
```
def line_search_combo(u,v,c_u,c_v,c_uv,n):
    u=np.asarray(u); v=np.asarray(v)
    full_t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,257)
    T=full_t[:,None]
    c = (1.0-T)**2 * c_u[None,:] + (2.0*T*(1.0-T)) * c_uv[None,:] + (T**2) * c_v[None,:]
    sc = 2.0*float(n)*c.max(axis=1)
    idx=int(np.argmin(sc)); best_t=float(full_t[idx]); best_sc=float(sc[idx])
    # local refine around top few
    top_idx = np.argsort(sc)[:5]
    for ti in top_idx:
       t0=float(full_t[ti])
       lo=max(0.0,t0-0.01); hi=min(1.0,t0+0.01)
       if hi<=lo: continue
       # if boundary? okay
       t2=np.linspace(lo,hi,33)
       T2=t2[:,None]
       c2=...
       sc2=...
       j=int(np.argmin(sc2)); t3=float(t2[j]); s3=float(sc2[j])
       if s3<best_sc: best_sc=s3; best_t=t3
    cand = (1.0-best_t)*u + best_t*v
    cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0); s=cand.sum(); if s<... cand uniform else cand/=s
    return best_t,cand,best_sc
```
Memory c 257*m; for n=2048 m=4095 ->257*4095*8=8.4MB, okay. For repeated LP iterations, allocate many but freed. Could reduce to 129 to save. Use 129 full and local refine. 129*4095=5.3MB. Good. Use 129.

### `get_lp_structure`
```
def get_lp_structure(n):
    if n in _LP_CACHE: return _LP_CACHE[n]
    if len(_LP_CACHE) >= 3: _LP_CACHE.clear()
    m=2*n-1
    T_indices = (np.tile(np.arange(n), n) + np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)).astype(np.int32)
    T_indptr = (np.arange(n+1, dtype=np.int64) * n)
    A_indices = np.concatenate((T_indices, np.arange(m, dtype=np.int32)))
    A_indptr = np.concatenate((T_indptr, np.array([n*n + m], dtype=np.int64)))
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    b_eq = np.ones(1)
    c_obj = np.zeros(n+1)
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(n)] + [(-np.inf, np.inf)]
    st = {'T_indices': T_indices, 'T_indptr': T_indptr, 'A_indices': A_indices, 'A_indptr': A_indptr, 'A_eq': A_eq, 'b_eq': b_eq, 'c': c_obj, 'bounds': bounds, 'm': m}
    _LP_CACHE[n]=st
    return st
```
List comprehension okay (not lambda). For n large, bounds list okay.
Memory: `T_indices` int32, `A_indices` int32. T_indptr int64, A_indptr int64. Good.

### `lp_best_response`
Global `_LP_PREF = [0]`? Use `_LP_MODE = [0]` where 0 auto,1 scipy,2 cvx,3 none.
```
_LP_MODE = [0]
def lp_best_response(a, timeout_sec=6.0):
    n=len(a); m=2*n-1
    st=get_lp_structure(n)
    # scipy if mode !=2
    if _LP_MODE[0] != 2:
       try:
          T_data=np.repeat(a, n)
          data=np.concatenate((T_data, -np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)))
          A=csc_matrix((data, st['A_indices'], st['A_indptr']), shape=(m, n+1))
          options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': float(timeout_sec), 'dual_feasibility_tolerance': 1e-8, 'dual_optimality_tolerance': 1e-8}
          res=spopt.linprog(st['c'], A_ub=A, A_eq=st['A_eq'], b_ub=np.zeros(m), b_eq=st['b_eq'], bounds=st['bounds'], method='highs', options=options)
          if res.x is not None:
              b=np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
              b=np.maximum(b,0.0); s=b.sum()
              if s > 1e-12 and np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
                  b/=s; _LP_MODE[0]=1; return b, float(res.x[-1])
          # if failed set mode2? if exception
       except Exception:
          _LP_MODE[0]=2
    # cvx fallback
    if _LP_MODE[0] != 3:
       try:
          import cvxpy as cp
          T_data=np.repeat(a,n)
          T=csc_matrix((T_data, st['T_indices'], st['T_indptr']), shape=(m,n))
          bvar=cp.Variable(n, nonneg=True); tvar=cp.Variable()
          prob=cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(tvar), [T @ bvar <= tvar, cp.sum(bvar) == 1])
          try: prob.solve(solver=cp.ECOS)
          except Exception: prob.solve()
          if bvar.value is not None:
             b=np.asarray(bvar.value,dtype=np.float64); b=np.maximum(b,0); s=b.sum(); if s>1e-12: b/=s; return b, float(tvar.value) if np.isfinite(tvar.value) else inf
          _LP_MODE[0]=3
       except Exception:
          _LP_MODE[0]=3
    return None, float('inf')
```
Potential: `st['c'], st['A_eq']` reused; linprog may modify? likely not. `np.zeros(m)` each call. Could cache zeros. Not necessary.
If scipy returns x but t is negative/nan, b still feasible? return. t not used much.
If first scipy fails due option, set mode2; if cvx not installed set3. Good.

**However**, `_LP_MODE` global list. If scipy works for n, then no cvx. If later n larger and scipy memory error, but mode1, it will still try scipy each time; if fails, mode2. Good.

**Time limit in options**: If `time_limit` option not accepted, exception per LP and set mode2, then cvx maybe. Good.

### `fictitious_play`
Implement with pool add.
```
def add_candidate(pool, cand, max_size=35):
    if pool is None: return
    cand=normalize_positive(cand)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(cand)): return
    sc=quick_score(cand)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return
    pool.append(cand)
    if len(pool) > max_size:
       scored=[]
       for idx,x in enumerate(pool):
          s=quick_score(x)
          scored.append((s, idx, x))
       scored.sort()
       keep = scored[:max_size//2]
       del pool[:]
       for _,_,x in keep:
          pool.append(x)
```
Every add quick_score; in FP add every 10, okay. In pool trim, quick_score again; okay.

`fictitious_play`:
```
def fictitious_play(a_start, budget, pool=None, label=''):
    a=normalize_positive(a_start); n=len(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=quick_score(a)
    active=a.copy(); avg=a.copy()
    t0=time.monotonic(); it=0; no_lp=0; initial_sc=best_sc
    if pool is not None: add_candidate(pool, best)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < budget:
       rem=budget-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 30: break
       it += 1
       if it > 20000: break
       lp_timeout = min(6.0, max(2.0, rem/10.0))
       b, tval = lp_best_response(active, timeout_sec=lp_timeout)
       if b is None:
           no_lp += 1
           if no_lp >= 4: break
           active, sc = subgradient_candidate(active)
           if sc < best_sc:
              best=active.copy(); best_sc=sc
           continue
       no_lp=0
       c_active=fast_autoconv(active)
       c_b=fast_autoconv(b)
       c_cross=fast_conv_pair(active,b)
       t1,cand1,sc1 = line_search_combo(active,b,c_active,c_b,c_cross,n)
       if sc1 < best_sc:
           best=cand1.copy(); best_sc=sc1
       # line search from best to b if different enough
       cand2=cand1
       if it % 2 == 0:
          if np.linalg.norm(best-active) < 1e-4? use norm maybe heavy; just do every 3? do each? cost.
          c_best=fast_autoconv(best)
          c_cross_best=fast_conv_pair(best,b)
          t2,cand2,sc2=line_search_combo(best,b,c_best,c_b,c_cross_best,n)
          if sc2 < best_sc:
              best=cand2.copy(); best_sc=sc2
       # mix update
       alpha = 2.0/(it + 3.0)
       mix = (1.0-alpha)*avg + alpha*b
       mix=normalize_positive(mix)
       sc_mix=quick_score(mix)
       # choose active among cand1/2, mix, best
       # get scores: sc1, sc2 maybe, sc_mix, best_sc
       if sc_mix < best_sc:
           best=mix.copy(); best_sc=sc_mix
       # active choose lowest among existing, maybe mix or best
       if sc_mix < (sc1 if cand2 is cand1 else min(sc1,sc2) if it%2==0 else sc1):
           active = mix.copy()
       else:
           # if best better than line cand, use best
           if best_sc <= min(sc1, (sc2 if it%2==0 else inf)):
               active = best.copy()
           else:
               active = (cand2 if (it%2==0 and sc2<sc1) else cand1).copy()
       avg=mix
       if pool is not None and it % 8 == 0:
           add_candidate(pool, active)
           add_candidate(pool, mix)
           add_candidate(pool, b)
       if label and it % 20 == 0:
           print(f"    FP {label} it={it} score={best_sc:.8f}")
    return best, best_sc, avg
```
Need care with `inf` variable. Use `float('inf')`. The active selection logic complex; can simplify: `candidates = [(sc1,cand1), (sc_mix,mix), (best_sc,best)]`; if did cand2 add (sc2,cand2). Choose min. Use list and sort? No lambda: `list.sort()` by first then? If first equal compare arrays? Bad. Use loop:
```
active_score=sc1; active_arr=cand1
if sc_mix < active_score: active_score=sc_mix; active_arr=mix
if best_sc < active_score: active_score=best_sc; active_arr=best
if did2 and sc2 < active_score: active_score=sc2; active_arr=cand2
active=active_arr
```
Good.
`did2 = (it % 2 == 0)` and sc2 initialized inf.
When best updated by mix, best_sc. Then candidates includes best.
After active choose, avg=mix.
If b has high score, mix maybe. Good.

One issue: `line_search_combo` returns cand normalized; if best equals active, c_active/c_best duplicate. Fine.

Could LP timeout side effect: If `lp_best_response` sets `_LP_MODE` to 3 after many failures, then no LP; subgradient fallback.

### `coord_prep`
Return tuple. Need S normalized? We'll pass S=a.sum() (approx1). Coordinates may have small negative? a nonneg.
```
def coord_prep(a, conv, S, i):
    n=len(a); v=float(a[i]); k_lo=i; k_hi=min(i+n-1, 2*n-2); n_aff=k_hi-k_lo+1
    seg=conv[k_lo:k_hi+1]
    partners=np.arange(n_aff, dtype=np.float64)
    c_lin = seg - 2.0*v*a[partners]
    has_quad = (2*i >= k_lo) and (2*i <= k_hi)
    c_quad = 0.0
    if has_quad:
        qidx = int(2*i - k_lo)
        c_quad = float(conv[2*i]) - v*v
        c_lin = c_lin.copy(); partners = partners.copy()
        c_lin[qidx] = -1e100
        partners[qidx] = 0.0
    else:
        qidx = -1
    unaff = -1e30
    if k_lo > 0:
        val=float(conv[:k_lo].max()); if val>unaff: unaff=val
    if k_hi < 2*n-2:
        val=float(conv[k_hi+1:].max()); if val>unaff: unaff=val
    params=(c_lin, partners, has_quad, c_quad, unaff, S, v, n)
    return params, k_lo, k_hi, has_quad, qidx
```
`partners` dtype float for multiplication, but also used as indices? For update we recompute integer partners. In c_lin, indexing `a[partners]` with float array not allowed! Need `int_partners = np.arange(n_aff)` for indexing, and `ap = int_partners.astype(float)` for multiplication. Use separate.
```
ip = np.arange(n_aff)
ap_float = ip.astype(np.float64)
c_lin = seg - 2.0*v*a[ip]
...
params=(c_lin, ap_float, ...)
```
For update, recompute `ip_update = np.arange(k_lo,k_hi+1)-i`.

`coord_score`:
```
def coord_score(x, params):
    c_lin, ap, has_quad, c_quad, unaff, S, v, n = params
    ns = S + x - v
    if ns < 1e-12: return 1e100
    m = unaff
    if c_lin.size:
        val = float(np.max(c_lin + 2.0 * ap * x))
        if val > m: m = val
    if has_quad:
        q = c_quad + x*x
        if q > m: m = q
    if m < 0: m = 0.0
    return float(2.0*n*m/(ns*ns))
```
If c_lin contains -1e100 and max negative, unaff maybe -1e30; m could -1e30 if only quad? has_quad q positive. If no quad, finite.

`golden_section_params`:
```
def golden_section_params(lo, hi, max_iter, params):
    if hi <= lo: return lo
    gr = (math.sqrt(5.0) - 1.0) / 2.0
    c = hi - gr*(hi-lo); d = lo + gr*(hi-lo)
    fc=coord_score(c,params); fd=coord_score(d,params)
    for _ in range(max_iter):
        if hi-lo < 1e-14*max(1.0, abs(c)): break
        if fc <= fd:
            hi=d; d=c; fd=fc; c=hi-gr*(hi-lo); fc=coord_score(c,params)
        else:
            lo=c; c=d; fc=fd; d=lo+gr*(hi-lo); fd=coord_score(d,params)
    return 0.5*(lo+hi)
```
Import math.

`exact_coord_descent`:
```
def exact_coord_descent(a0, time_limit, rng=None, label='', verbose=False):
    a=normalize_positive(a0); n=len(a)
    if n<2 or time_limit < 1: return a, quick_score(a)
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    conv=fast_autoconv(a); S=float(a.sum()); cur_sc=quick_score(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_sc
    t0=time.monotonic(); sweeps=0; no_improve=0
    max_iter = 22 if n > 1024 else 30
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        sweeps += 1
        perm = rng.permutation(n)
        improved = False
        tol = 1e-14
        for i in perm:
            if time.monotonic()-t0 > time_limit: break
            v = float(a[i])
            if v < 1e-11: continue
            params,k_lo,k_hi,has_quad,qidx=coord_prep(a,conv,S,i)
            upper = max(5.0*v, 10.0/n)
            if upper > 1.0: upper = 1.0
            if upper <= v*1e-6: continue
            best_x = golden_section_params(0.0, upper, max_iter, params)
            new_sc = coord_score(best_x, params)
            # also evaluate current v? maybe no
            if new_sc < cur_sc - 1e-15:
                delta = best_x - v
                # update a
                a[i] = best_x
                S += delta
                if S < 1e-6:
                   # recompute
                   a=normalize_positive(a); S=float(a.sum()); conv=fast_autoconv(a); cur_sc=quick_score(a)
                   break
                ip_update = np.arange(k_lo, k_hi+1) - i
                changes = 2.0 * delta * a[ip_update]
                if has_quad:
                    changes = changes.copy()
                    changes[qidx] = delta * (2.0*v + delta)
                conv[k_lo:k_hi+1] += changes
                cur_sc = new_sc
                if cur_sc < best_sc - 1e-14:
                    best_sc = cur_sc; best = a.copy()
                improved = True
        if sweeps % 5 == 0:
            a=normalize_positive(a); S=float(a.sum()); conv=fast_autoconv(a); cur_sc=quick_score(a)
            if cur_sc < best_sc:
                best_sc=cur_sc; best=a.copy()
        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve >= 3: break
        else:
            no_improve = 0
        if verbose and sweeps % 10 == 0:
            print(f"    ECD {label} sweep={sweeps} score={best_sc:.8f}")
    final_sc=quick_score(best)
    return best, final_sc
```
Potential bug: In update, after setting `a[i]=best_x`, `a[ip_update]` includes updated a[i] for partner i when quad? For non-quad, if ip_update contains i? Only when k=2i, which is quad excluded? But ip_update includes i at qidx. For has_quad, we override changes at qidx, so using updated a[i] in linear changes for qidx doesn't matter after override. For other k, partner i may appear? Coordinate i appears in c_k for k where k-i=i? only k=2i. So no. For k where partner index equals i? only qidx. Thus okay. But for has_quad, `changes = 2.0*delta*a[ip_update]` uses updated a[i]=best_x at qidx; override. Good.
If `best_x` very close to v, delta small.
`upper` if v=0 skipped. If coordinate zero but could grow, we skip. Maybe allow a random fraction of zero coords: if v <1e-11 and rng.random()<0.02: upper=10/n. But not necessary? Could help introduce mass. Add:
```
if v < 1e-11:
    if rng.random() < 0.05: upper=10.0/n
    else: continue
else: upper=...
```
But if leading/trailing zeros, introducing mass may hurt. It's okay.
When v=0, S unchanged? delta=best_x. `delta*(2v+delta)=delta^2` correct.
Search upper for v=0 =10/n. Good.

`cur_sc` from `new_sc` might differ from `2n*conv.max()/S^2` due unaff? It is full score. Use it. But after many updates, conv may drift; recompute.

### `optimize_weights`
```
def optimize_weights(pool, n, budget, label=''):
    if not pool: return None, inf
    # interpolate and stack
    arrs=[]
    for a in pool[:40]:
       b=interpolate_seq(a,n)
       arrs.append(b)
    # score and sort
    scored=[]
    for idx,a in enumerate(arrs):
       sc=quick_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc, idx, a))
    if not scored: return None, inf
    scored.sort()
    picked=[]
    for sc,idx,a in scored:
       if len(picked)>=10: break
       if len(picked)>40: break? We have <=40.
       diverse=True
       for b in picked:
          if float(np.dot(a,b)) > 0.995:
             diverse=False; break
       if diverse: picked.append(a)
    P=len(picked)
    if P==1: return picked[0], quick_score(picked[0])
    A=np.stack(picked, axis=0)
    m=2*n-1
    C=np.zeros((P,P,m), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(P):
       for j in range(i,P):
          cij=fast_conv_pair(A[i], A[j])
          C[i,j]=cij
          C[j,i]=cij
    lam=np.ones(P)/P
    best_lam=lam.copy()
    best_a=normalize_positive(A @ lam) ; best_sc=quick_score(best_a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); sweeps=0; no_improve=0
    factors=(0.0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.25,1.5,2.0)
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < budget:
       sweeps+=1
       improved=False
       for i in range(P):
          if time.monotonic()-t0 > budget: break
          base=lam
          for f in factors:
             cand=base.copy(); cand[i]=base[i]*f; ss=cand.sum()
             if ss<1e-12: continue
             cand/=ss
             c=np.einsum('i,ijk,j->k', cand, C, cand, optimize=True)
             sc=2.0*n*float(c.max())
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-14:
                best_sc=sc; best_lam=cand; best_a=normalize_positive(A @ cand); improved=True; lam=cand.copy()
                base=cand # use updated base for next factors? But changing i based on new lam okay.
       if sweeps % 5 ==0:
          # no need recompute
       if not improved:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>=4: break
       else: no_improve=0
    if label: print(f"    WEIGHTS {label}: {best_sc:.8f}")
    return best_a, best_sc
```
Issue: In factor loop, `base=lam` reference; after updating lam to cand, `base=cand`, subsequent factors use updated base; okay. But for a given i, after improvement, trying further factors from new base may sequence. Good. But `cand=base.copy()` for f=1? We don't include 1.0; if base changed, no. If factor 0 and base[i]=0, cand same; skip? ss okay.
`A @ cand` uses matrix multiplication. Good.
`C` symmetric. `_LP_CACHE` maybe memory plus C large. If n=2048,P=10,m4095,C 100*4095*8=3.3MB, fine. For P=12, 4.8MB.

**Potential issue**: `np.einsum` each candidate with C large; coordinate optimize may be slow but P small. Good.

### `small_n_search`
Use helpers.
```
def small_n_search(n, budget, rng):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    if budget < 10: return None
    inits=make_inits(n,None,rng)
    scored=[]
    for idx,a in enumerate(inits):
       sc=quick_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc, idx, a))
    if not scored: return None
    scored.sort()
    best_a=scored[0][2]; best_sc=scored[0][0]
    t0=time.monotonic? already.
    for pos in range(1, min(4,len(scored))):
       if time.monotonic()-t0 > budget*0.7: break
       a_c=scored[pos][2]
       # CD short
       a_c,sc_c=exact_coord_descent(a_c, max(3, min(10,(budget/4))), rng)
       if sc_c < best_sc: best_a=a_c; best_sc=sc_c
       a_c,sc_l=l_bfgs_polish(a_c, max(3, min(15,(budget/2))), beta_targets=(4.0,10.0))
       if sc_l < best_sc: best_a=a_c; best_sc=sc_l
       a_c,sc_c=exact_coord_descent(a_c, max(3, min(15,(budget/2))), rng)
       if sc_c < best_sc: best_a=a_c; best_sc=sc_c
    return best_a, best_sc
```
No lambda. Time management rough. For n=64, CD/LBFGS fast.

### `basin_hopping`
Could write helper to use in propose.
```
def basin_hopping(a0, budget, rng, pool=None, label=''):
    a=normalize_positive(a0); n=len(a); best=a; best_sc=quick_score(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    sigmas=(0.005,0.01,0.02,0.03,0.05,0.08)
    done=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < budget:
       rem=budget-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem < 45: break
       pol_b = min(60.0, max(25.0, rem*0.25))
       r=rng.random()
       if r < 0.4:
          sigma=sigmas[int(rng.integers(0,len(sigmas)))]
          pert=a + rng.standard_normal(n)*sigma
       elif r < 0.7 and pool:
          other=pool[int(rng.integers(0,len(pool)))]
          alpha=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8))
          pert=alpha*a + (1-alpha)*interpolate_seq(other,n)
       else:
          # peak perturb
          conv=fast_autoconv(a); K=min(10,conv.size); peaks=np.argpartition(conv,-K)[-K:]
          pert=a.copy()
          for k in peaks:
             k=int(k)
             i_st=max(0,k-n+1); i_en=min(n-1,k)
             idxs=np.arange(i_st,i_en+1)
             if len(idxs)==0: continue
             picks=rng.choice(idxs,size=max(1,len(idxs)//5),replace=False)
             for ii in picks:
                pert[ii] *= (1.0 + rng.standard_normal()*0.1)
       pert=np.maximum(pert,0.0); s=pert.sum(); if s<1e-12: continue
       pert=pert/s
       a_opt, s_opt = l_bfgs_polish(pert, pol_b, beta_targets=(5.0,12.0), label=label)
       if s_opt < best_sc:
          best=a_opt; best_sc=s_opt
       # quick CD if time
       rem=budget-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if rem>30:
          a_cd, s_cd = exact_coord_descent(best, min(30.0, rem*0.5), rng, label=label)
          if s_cd < best_sc:
             best=a_cd; best_sc=s_cd
       done+=1
    return best,best_sc
```
This may be expensive: each basin polygon L-BFGS 25-60s, so at most 6 loops in 300s. Good. Peak perturb uses `rng.choice` with possible idx dtype. `k` from conv length 2n-1; coordinate pairs for standard conv? For peak k, affected coordinates j such that 0<=k-j<n -> j in [max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)]. Good.

Need helper `polish_sequence` top-level:
```
def polish_sequence(a0, budget, rng, label=''):
    t0=time.monotonic(); n=len(a0)
    a=s=a0, score
    if budget < 20: return a,score
    lb_b = min(budget*0.45, 180.0)
    if lb_b >= 10:
       a,s = l_bfgs_polish(a, lb_b, beta_targets=(4.0,10.0,20.0), label=label)
    rem = budget - (time.monotonic()-t0)
    if rem > 15:
       a2,s2 = exact_coord_descent(a, rem, rng, label=label)
